128: static int sensor_cmp(void *a_, const void *b_)
129: {
130: 	struct sensor *a = a_;
131: 	const struct sensor *b = b_;
132: 	if (a->key.package_no < b->key.package_no)
133: 		return -1;
134: 	if (a->key.package_no > b->key.package_no)
135: 		return 1;
136: 	if (a->key.sensor_no < b->key.sensor_no)
137: 		return -1;
138: 	if (a->key.sensor_no > b->key.sensor_no)
139: 		return 1;
140: 	return 0;
141: }
142: 
143: static struct rbt sensor_tree = RBT_INITIALIZER(sensor_cmp);
144: static regex_t sensor_re;
145: static const char *sensor_str =
146: 	"(\\/sys\\/devices\\/platform\\/coretemp.){1}([0-9]+){1}(\\/hwmon){1}"
147: 	"(\\/hwmon){1}([0-9]+){1}(\\/temp){1}([0-9]+){1}(_label)";

151: int create_sensors_cb(const char *fpath, const struct stat *sb, int typeflag, struct FTW *ftwbuf)
152: {
153: 	int rc;
154: 	regmatch_t match[16];
155: 	char path[PATH_MAX];
156: 	if (!strstr(fpath, "coretemp"))
157: 		return 0;
158: 	switch (typeflag) {
159: 	case FTW_F:  /* fpath is a regular file. */
160: 		rc = regexec(&sensor_re, fpath, 16, match, 0);
161: 		if (!rc) {
162: 			struct sensor *sensor = calloc(1, sizeof *sensor);
163: 			sensor->key.package_no = strtod(&fpath[match[2].rm_so], NULL);
164: 			sensor->key.sensor_no =	strtod(&fpath[match[7].rm_so], NULL);
165: 			char *dirn = strdup(fpath);
166: 			dirn = dirname(dirn);
167: 			sprintf(path, "%s/temp%d_label",  dirn, sensor->key.sensor_no);
168: 			sensor->temp_label_path = strdup(path);
169: 			sprintf(path, "%s/temp%d_max", dirn, sensor->key.sensor_no);
170: 			sensor->temp_max_path = strdup(path);
171: 			sprintf(path, "%s/temp%d_crit", dirn, sensor->key.sensor_no);
172: 			sensor->temp_crit_path = strdup(path);
173: 			sprintf(path, "%s/temp%d_crit_alarm", dirn, sensor->key.sensor_no);
174: 			sensor->temp_crit_alarm_path = strdup(path);
175: 			sprintf(path, "%s/temp%d_input", dirn, sensor->key.sensor_no);
176: 			sensor->temp_input_path = strdup(path);
177: 			rbn_init(&sensor->rbn, &sensor->key.package_no);
178: 			rbt_ins(&sensor_tree, &sensor->rbn);
179: 			free(dirn);
180: 		}
181: 		break;
182: 	case FTW_D:  /* fpath is a directory. */
183: 		break;
184: 	case FTW_DNR: /* fpath is a directory which can't be read. */
185: 	case FTW_NS: /* The stat(2) call failed on fpath, which is not a symbolic link. */
186: 	case FTW_SL: /* Symbolic link.  */
187: 	default:
188: 		break;
189: 	}
190: 	return 0;
191: }

254: static int create_metric_set(base_data_t base)
255: {
256: 	int rc;

258: 	struct rbn *rbn;
259: 	struct sensor *sens;
260: 
261: 	/* Create a metric set of the required size */
262: 	schema = base_schema_new(base);
263: 	if (!schema) {
264: 		ovis_log(mylog, OVIS_LERROR,
265: 		       "The schema '%s' could not be created, errno=%d.\n",
266: 		       base->schema_name, errno);
267: 		rc = EINVAL;
268: 		goto err;
269: 	}
270: 
271: 	/*
272: 	 * Walk the /sys/devices/platform/coretemp.[package#]/hpmon/hwmon[package#]
273: 	 * path to count the number of sensors and add them to a tree sorted by
274: 	 * package_no, sensor_no
275: 	 */
276: 	rc = regcomp(&sensor_re, sensor_str, REG_EXTENDED);
277: 	rc = nftw("/sys/devices/platform", create_sensors_cb, 16, FTW_PHYS);
278: 	if (rc) {
279: 		ovis_log(mylog, OVIS_LERROR,
280: 		       "Error %d creating the sensor tree.\n", rc);
281: 		goto err;
282: 
283: 	}
284: 
285: 	/* Walk the tree and open all the /sys files need to read the
286: 	 * sensor data */
287: 	collect_sensors();
288: 
289: 	ldms_record_t core_def =
290: 		ldms_record_from_template("core_temps",
291: 					  core_template, core_metric_ids);
292: 	size_t heap_sz = rbt_card(&sensor_tree) * ldms_record_heap_size_get(core_def);
293: 
294: 	/* Add record definition into the schema */
295: 	core_rec_idx = ldms_schema_record_add(schema, core_def);
296: 	if (core_rec_idx < 0) {
297: 		rc = -core_rec_idx;
298: 		goto err;
299: 	}
300: 
301: 	/* Add a list of cores */
302: 	core_list_idx = ldms_schema_metric_list_add(schema, "core_list", NULL, heap_sz);
303: 	if (core_list_idx < 0) {
304: 		rc = -core_list_idx;
305: 		goto err;
306: 	}
307: 
308: 	set = base_set_new(base);
309: 	if (!set) {
310: 		ovis_log(mylog, OVIS_LERROR, "Error %d creating the metric set.\n", errno);
311: 		rc = errno;
312: 		goto err;
313: 	}
314: 
315: 	/* Populate the list with a record for each core */
316: 	ldms_mval_t core_v, core_list_v;
317: 	core_list_v = ldms_metric_get(set, core_list_idx);
318: 	RBT_FOREACH(rbn, &sensor_tree) {
319: 		core_v = ldms_record_alloc(set, core_rec_idx);
320: 		sens = container_of(rbn, struct sensor, rbn);
321: 		sens->core_v = core_v;
322: 		ldms_record_set_u32(core_v, PACKAGE_NO_IDX, sens->key.package_no);
323: 		ldms_record_set_u32(core_v, SENSOR_NO_IDX, sens->key.sensor_no);
324: 		ldms_record_array_set_str(core_v, LABEL_IDX, sens->temp_label);
325: 		ldms_record_set_float(core_v, CRIT_TEMP_IDX, sens->temp_crit);
326: 		ldms_record_set_float(core_v, MAX_TEMP_IDX, sens->temp_max);
327: 		ldms_record_set_float(core_v, CRIT_TEMP_IDX, sens->temp_crit);
328: 		ldms_record_set_float(core_v, CRIT_ALARM_IDX, sens->temp_crit_alarm);
329: 		rc = ldms_list_append_record(set, core_list_v, core_v);
330: 	}
331:  err:
332: 	return rc;
333: }

1: .. _coretemp:
2: 
3: ===============
4: coretemp
5: ===============
6: 
7: ---------------------------------------------------------
8: An LDMS sampler plugin that monitors CPU temperature data
9: ---------------------------------------------------------
10: 
11: :Date:   3 May 2022
12: :Manual section: 7
13: :Manual group: LDMS sampler
14: 
15: 
16: SYNOPSIS
17: ========
18: 
19: | Within ldmsd_controller or a configuration file:
20: | load name=coretemp config name=coretemp producer=<name>
21:   instance=<name> component_id=<int>
22: 
23: DESCRIPTION
24: ===========
25: 
26: The coretemp sampler collects information from the Linux coretemp module
27: through files located in /sys/devices/platform. Files in this directory
28: are walked recursively and regular expressions are used to select
29: entries produced by the Linux coretemp module.
30: 
31: See the Linux :ref:`modprobe(8) <modprobe>` command for information on how to load Linux
32: modules.
33: 
34: CONFIGURATION ATTRIBUTE SYNTAX
35: ==============================
36: 
37: See man base.
38: 
39: EXAMPLES
40: ========
41: 
42: Within ldmsd_controller or a configuration file:
43: 
44: ::
45: 
46:    load name=coretemp
47:    config name=coretempp producer=vm1_1 instance=vm1_1/coretemp
48:    start name=coretemp interval=1000000 offset=0
49: 
50: SEE ALSO
51: ========
52: 
53: :ref:`ldmsd(8) <ldmsd>`, :ref:`ldms_quickstart(7) <ldms_quickstart>`, :ref:`ldmsd_controller(8) <ldmsd_controller>`, :ref:`ldms_sampler_base(7) <ldms_sampler_base>`,
54: :ref:`filesingle(7) <filesingle>`

## Assistant

_Thinking:_

Let me analyze the code to understand why `core_list` is empty.

The sampler:
1. In `create_metric_set`, it calls `nftw("/sys/devices/platform", create_sensors_cb, 16, FTW_PHYS)` to walk the sysfs tree and find coretemp sensor files matching the regex `sensor_str`:

```
"(\\/sys\\/devices\\/platform\\/coretemp.){1}([0-9]+){1}(\\/hwmon){1}"
"(\\/hwmon){1}([0-9]+){1}(\\/temp){1}([0-9]+){1}(_label)"
```

So the regex expects paths like `/sys/devices/platform/coretemp.<N>/hwmon/hwmon<M>/temp<L>_label`.

2. `create_sensors_cb` first checks `if (!strstr(fpath, "coretemp")) return 0;`.

Key things to check:

The user gets an empty `core_list` but the set is created successfully (schema contains record type and list). That means the sensor tree was empty (no matches), so `RBT_FOREACH` loop populated nothing. Actually wait — if the tree is empty, `heap_sz = 0`, and the list is empty. The set gets created fine. So the walk found no files matching the regex.

Possible causes:
1. On newer kernels (e.g., RHEL 9 / SLES 15 SP5+, kernel 5.14+? Actually since kernel 5.9 or so? Let me recall), the coretemp hwmon device path changed. ## [coretemp ](https://ovis-hpc.readthedocs.io/projects/ldms/en/latest/rst_man/src/sampler/coretemp/ldms-sampler_coretemp.html)

# coretemp 

## An LDMS sampler plugin that monitors CPU temperature data 

Date:

3 May 2022

Manual section:

7

Manual group:

LDMS sampler

### SYNOPSIS 

Within ldmsd_controller or a configuration file:

load name=coretemp config name=coretemp producer=\ instance=\ component_id=\

### DESCRIPTION 

The coretemp sampler collects information from the Linux coretemp module through files located in /sys/devices/platform. Files in this directory are walked recursively and regular expressions are used to select entries produced by the Linux coretemp module.

See the Linux modprobe(8) command for information on how to load Linux modules.

### CONFIGURATION ATTRIBUTE SYNTAX 

See man base.

### EXAMPLES 

Within ldmsd_controller or a configuration file:

```
load name=coretemp
config name=coretempp producer=vm1_1 instance=vm1_1/coretemp
start name=coretemp interval=1000000 offset=0

```

## [Kernel driver coretemp — The Linux Kernel  documentation](https://docs.kernel.org/hwmon/coretemp.html)

Prefix: ‘coretemp’

...

## Description¶

This driver permits reading the DTS (Digital Temperature Sensor) embedded inside Intel CPUs. This driver can read both the per-core and per-package temperature using the appropriate sensors. The per-package sensor is available on Sandy Bridge and all newer processors. The driver will show the temperature of all cores inside a package under a single device directory inside hwmon.

Temperature is measured in degrees Celsius and measurement resolution is 1 degree C. Valid temperatures are from 0 to TjMax degrees C, because the actual value of temperature register is in fact a delta from TjMax.

Temperature known as TjMax is the maximum junction temperature of processor, which depends on the CPU model. See table below. At this temperature, protection mechanism will perform actions to forcibly cool down the processor. Alarm may be raised, if the temperature grows enough (more than TjMax) to trigger the Out-Of-Spec bit. The following table summarizes the exported sysfs files:

Per-core sysfs entries are numbered core_id + 2 (represented here by ‘X’). The package entries, if available, are always temp1_*.

tempX_input

Core temperature (in millidegrees Celsius). Reading fails with -ENODATA while the CPU reports the temperature as invalid. Some CPUs do this when the temperature is too low to be measured.

tempX_max

Maximum recommended operating temperature (Tcontrol). All cooling devices should be turned on.

tempX_crit

Maximum junction temperature (in millidegrees Celsius).

tempX_crit_alarm

Set when Out-of-spec bit is set, never clears. Correct CPU operation is no longer guaranteed.

tempX_label

Contains string “Core N”, where N is the core_id (X - 2), as shown in /sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu*/topology/core_id. This is not the logical CPU number, and core_id values may be sparse. For Package temp, this will be “Package id Y”, where Y is the package number.

On modern CPUs (Nehalem and newer), TjMax is read from the MSR_IA32_TEMPERATURE_TARGET register. On older models without this MSR, TjMax is determined using lookup tables or heuristics. If these don’t work for your CPU, you can pass the correct TjMax value as a module parameter (tjmax).

## [[PATCH V2 02/11] hwmon: (coretemp) Fix bogus core_id to attr name mapping — Linux Hardware Monitor](https://www.spinics.net/lists/linux-hwmon/msg23088.html)

# [PATCH V2 02/11] hwmon: (coretemp) Fix bogus core_id to attr name mapping

- To: linux@xxxxxxxxxxxx, jdelvare@xxxxxxxx
- Subject: [PATCH V2 02/11] hwmon: (coretemp) Fix bogus core_id to attr name mapping
- From: Zhang Rui <rui.zhang@xxxxxxxxx>
- Date: Fri, 2 Feb 2024 17:21:35 +0800
- Cc: fenghua.yu@xxxxxxxxx, linux-hwmon@xxxxxxxxxxxxxxx, linux-kernel@xxxxxxxxxxxxxxx
- In-reply-to: < 20240202092144.71180-1-rui.zhang@intel.com>
- References: < 20240202092144.71180-1-rui.zhang@intel.com>

```
Before commit 7108b80a542b ("hwmon/coretemp: Handle large core ID
value"), there is a fixed mapping between
1. cpu_core_id
2. the index in pdata->core_data[] array
3. the sysfs attr name, aka "tempX_"
The later two always equal cpu_core_id + 2.

After the commit, pdata->core_data[] index is got from ida so that it
can handle sparse core ids and support more cores within a package.

However, the commit erroneously maps the sysfs attr name to
pdata->core_data[] index instead of cpu_core_id + 2.

As a result, the code is not aligned with the comments, and brings user
visible changes in hwmon sysfs on systems with sparse core id.

For example, before commit 7108b80a542b ("hwmon/coretemp: Handle large
core ID value"),
/sys/class/hwmon/hwmon2/temp2_label:Core 0
/sys/class/hwmon/hwmon2/temp3_label:Core 1
/sys/class/hwmon/hwmon2/temp4_label:Core 2
/sys/class/hwmon/hwmon2/temp5_label:Core 3
/sys/class/hwmon/hwmon2/temp6_label:Core 4
/sys/class/hwmon/hwmon3/temp10_label:Core 8
/sys/class/hwmon/hwmon3/temp11_label:Core 9
after commit,
/sys/class/hwmon/hwmon2/temp2_label:Core 0
/sys/class/hwmon/hwmon2/temp3_label:Core 1
/sys/class/hwmon/hwmon2/temp4_label:Core 2
/sys/class/hwmon/hwmon2/temp5_label:Core 3
/sys/class/hwmon/hwmon2/temp6_label:Core 4
/sys/class/hwmon/hwmon2/temp7_label:Core 8
/sys/class/hwmon/hwmon2/temp8_label:Core 9

Restore the previous behavior and rework the code, comments and variable
names to avoid future confusions.

Fixes: 7108b80a542b ("hwmon/coretemp: Handle large core ID value")
Signed-off-by: Zhang Rui <rui.zhang@xxxxxxxxx>

...

+		/*
+		 * We map the attr number to core id of the CPU
+		 * The attr number is always core id + 2
+		 * The Pkgtemp will always show up as temp1_*, if available
+		 */
+		int attr_no = tdata->is_pkg_data ? 1 : tdata->cpu_core_id + 2;
+
 		snprintf(tdata->attr_name[i], CORETEMP_NAME_LENGTH,
 			 "temp%d_%s", attr_no, suffixes[i]);

...

+	 * tdata for core: pdata->core_data[2] ..

## [drivers/hwmon/coretemp.c](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/master/drivers/hwmon/coretemp.c)

/*
 * Per-Core Temperature Data
 * @temp_valid: Whether the CPU reported @temp as valid. @temp is not
 * reported when this is false.
 * @tjmax: The static tjmax value when tjmax cannot be retrieved from
 * IA32_TEMPERATURE_TARGET MSR.
 * @last_updated: The time when the current temperature value was updated
 * earlier (in jiffies).
 * @cpu_core_id: The CPU Core from which temperature values should be read
 * This value is passed as "id" field to rdmsr/wrmsr functions.
 * @status_reg: One of IA32_THERM_STATUS or IA32_PACKAGE_THERM_STATUS,
 * from where the temperature values should be read.
 * @attr_size: Number of sysfs attributes in this temperature data group.
 */
struct temp_data {
 int temp;
 bool temp_valid;
 int tjmax;
 u64 last_updated;
 unsigned int cpu;
 int index;
 u32 cpu_core_id;
 u32 status_reg;
 int attr_size;
 struct device_attribute sd_attrs[TOTAL_ATTRS];
 char attr_name[TOTAL_ATTRS][CORETEMP_NAME_LENGTH];
 struct attribute *attrs[TOTAL_ATTRS + 1];
 struct attribute_group attr_group;
 struct mutex update_lock;

...

static ssize_t show_label(struct device *dev,
 struct device_attribute *devattr, char *buf)
{
 struct platform_data *pdata = dev_get_drvdata(dev);
 struct temp_data *tdata = container_of(devattr, struct temp_data, sd_attrs[ATTR_LABEL]);

 if (is_pkg_temp_data(tdata))
 return sprintf(buf, "Package id %u\n", pdata->pkg_id);

 return sprintf(buf, "Core %u\n", tdata->cpu_core_id);
}

...

static ssize_t show_temp(struct device *dev,
 struct device_attribute *devattr, char *buf)
{
 struct msr val;
 struct temp_data *tdata = container_of(devattr, struct temp_data, sd_attrs[ATTR_TEMP]);
 int tjmax, temp;
 bool valid;

 mutex_lock(&tdata->update_lock);

 /* Check whether the time interval has elapsed */
 if (time_after64(get_jiffies_64(), tdata->last_updated + HZ)) {
 tjmax = get_tjmax(tdata, dev);
 rdmsrq_on_cpu(tdata->cpu, tdata->status_reg, &val.q);
 /*
 * Bit 31 indicates whether the reading is valid. Some CPUs
 * clear it while the temperature is too low to be measured.
 * Such a reading is not a temperature. Do not report it.
 */
 tdata->temp_valid = (val.l >> 31) & 1;
 tdata->temp = tjmax - ((val.l >> 16) & 0xff) * 1000;
 tdata->last_updated = get_jiffies_64();
 }

 valid = tdata->temp_valid;
 temp = tdata->temp;
 mutex_unlock(&tdata->update_lock);

 if (!valid)
 return -ENODATA;
 return sprintf(buf, "%d\n", temp);
}

static int create_core_attrs(struct temp_data *tdata, struct device *dev)
{
 static ssize_t (*const rd_ptr[TOTAL_ATTRS]) (struct device *dev,
 struct device_attribute *devattr, char *buf) = {
 show_label, show_crit_alarm, show_temp, show_tjmax,
 show_ttarget };
 static const char *const suffixes[TOTAL_ATTRS] = {
 "label", "crit_alarm", "input", "crit", "max"
 };
 int i;

 for (i = 0; i < tdata->attr_size; i++) {
 /*
 * We map the attr number to core id of the CPU
 * The attr number is always core id + 2
 * The Pkgtemp will always show up as temp1_*, if available
 */
 int attr_no = is_pkg_temp_data(tdata) ? 1 : tdata->cpu_core_id + 2;

 snprintf(tdata->attr_name[i], CORETEMP_NAME_LENGTH,
 "temp%d_%s", attr_no, suffixes[i]);
 sysfs_attr_init(&tdata->sd_attrs[i].attr);
 tdata->sd_attrs[i].attr.name = tdata->attr_name[i];
 tdata->sd_attrs[i].attr.mode = 0444;
 tdata->sd_attrs[i].show = rd_ptr[i];
 tdata->attrs[i] = &tdata->sd_attrs[i].attr;
 }
 tdata->attr_group.attrs = tdata->attrs;
 return sysfs_create_group(&dev->kobj, &tdata->attr_group);
}

...

static struct temp_data *
init_temp_data(struct platform_data *pdata, unsigned int cpu, int pkg_flag)
{
 struct temp_data *tdata;

 if (!pdata->core_data) {
 int nr_cores = topology_num_cores_per_package();

 pdata->core_data = kzalloc_objs(struct temp_data *, nr_cores);
 if (!pdata->core_data)
 return NULL;
 pdata->nr_cores = nr_cores;
 }

 tdata = kzalloc_obj(struct temp_data);
 if (!tdata)
 return NULL;

 if (pkg_flag) {
 pdata->pkg_data = tdata;
 /* Use tdata->index as indicator of package temp data */
 tdata->index = -1;
 } else {
 tdata->index = ida_alloc_max(&pdata->ida, pdata->nr_cores - 1, GFP_KERNEL);
 if (tdata->index < 0) {
 kfree(tdata);
 return NULL;
 }
 pdata->core_data[tdata->index] = tdata;
 }

 tdata->status_reg = pkg_flag ? MSR_IA32_PACKAGE_THERM_STATUS :
 MSR_IA32_THERM_STATUS;
 tdata->cpu = cpu;
 tdata->cpu_core_id = topology_core_id(cpu);
 tdata->attr_size = MAX_CORE_ATTRS;
 /* Backdate to force a refresh on the first read */
 tdata->last_updated = get_jiffies_64() - HZ - 1;
 mutex_init(&tdata->update_lock);
 return tdata;
}

...

static struct temp_data *get_temp_data(struct platform_data *pdata, int cpu)
{
 int i;

 /* cpu < 0 means get pkg temp_data */
 if (cpu < 0)
 return pdata->pkg_data;

 for (i = 0; i < pdata->nr_cores; i++) {
 if (pdata->core_data[i] &&
 pdata->core_data[i]->cpu_core_id == topology_core_id(cpu))
 return pdata->core_data[i];
 }
 return NULL;
}

static int create_core_data(struct platform_device *pdev, unsigned int cpu,
 int pkg_flag)
{
 struct temp_data *tdata;
 struct platform_data *pdata = platform_get_drvdata(pdev);
 struct cpuinfo_x86 *c = &cpu_data(cpu);
 u64 val;
 int err;

 tdata = init_temp_data(pdata, cpu, pkg_flag);
 if (!tdata)
 return -ENOMEM;

...

tdata->attr_size++;

 /* Create sysfs interfaces */
 err = create_core_attrs(tdata, pdata->hwmon_dev);
 if (err)
 goto err;

 return 0;

## [Naming and data format standards for sysfs files — The Linux Kernel  documentation](https://docs.kernel.org/hwmon/sysfs-interface.html)

An alternative method that some programs use is to access the sysfs files directly. This document briefly describes the standards that the drivers follow, so that an application program can scan for entries and access this data in a simple and consistent way. That said, such programs will have to implement conversion, labeling and hiding of inputs. For this reason, it is still not recommended to bypass the library.

Each chip gets its own directory in the sysfs /sys/devices tree. To find all sensor chips, it is easier to follow the device symlinks from /sys/class/hwmon/hwmon*.

Up to lm-sensors 3.0.0, libsensors looks for hardware monitoring attributes in the “physical” device directory. Since lm-sensors 3.0.1, attributes found in the hwmon “class” device directory are also supported. Complex drivers (e.g. drivers for multifunction chips) may want to use this possibility to avoid namespace pollution. The only drawback will be that older versions of libsensors won’t support the driver in question.

All sysfs values are fixed point numbers.

There is only one value per file, unlike the older /proc specification. The common scheme for files naming is: _. Usual types for sensor chips are “in” (voltage), “temp” (temperature) and “fan” (fan). Usual items are “input” (measured value), “max” (high threshold), “min” (low threshold). Numbering usually starts from 1, except for voltages which start from 0 (because most data sheets use this). A number is always used for elements that can be present more than once, even if there is a single element of the given type on the specific chip. Other files do not refer to a specific element, so they have a simple name, and no number.

...

## Attribute access¶

Hardware monitoring sysfs attributes are displayed by unrestricted userspace applications. For this reason, all standard ABI attributes shall be world readable. Writeable standard ABI attributes shall be writeable only for privileged users.

[0-*]

denotes any positive number starting from 0

[1-*]

denotes any positive number starting from 1

RO

read only value

WO

write only value

RW

read/write value

Read/write values may be read-only for some chips, depending on the hardware implementation.

All entries (except name) are optional, and should only be created in a given driver if the chip has the feature.

...

### Temperatures¶

temp[1-*]_type
: Sensor type selection.
temp[1-*]_max
: Temperature max value.
temp[1-*]_min
: Temperature min value.
temp[1-*]_max_hyst
: Temperature hysteresis value for max limit.
temp[1-*]_min_hyst
: Temperature hysteresis value for min limit.
temp[1-*]_input
: Temperature input value.
temp[1-*]_crit
: Temperature critical max value, typically greater than corresponding temp_max values.
temp[1-*]_crit_hyst
: Temperature hysteresis value for critical limit.

...

temp[1-*]_emergency_hyst
: Temperature hysteresis value

...

temp[1-*]_lcrit
: Temperature critical min value, typically lower than corresponding temp_min values.
temp[1-*]_lcrit_hyst
: Temperature hysteresis value for critical min limit.
temp[1-*]_offset
: Temperature offset which is added to the temperature reading by the chip.
temp[1-*]_label
: Suggested temperature channel label.
temp[1-*]_lowest
: Historical minimum temperature
temp[1-*]_highest
: Historical maximum temperature
temp[1-*]_reset_history
: Reset temp_lowest and temp_highest
temp_reset_history
: Reset temp_lowest and temp_highest for all sensors
temp[1-*]_enable
: Enable or disable the sensors.
temp[1-*]_rated_min
: Minimum rated temperature.
temp[1-*]_rated_max
: Maximum rated temperature.

## [https://raw.githubusercontent.com/edgarsilva/omarchy-hw-monitor/main/hw-probe](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/edgarsilva/omarchy-hw-monitor/main/hw-probe)

# Discovers which sysfs files this machine exposes for CPU and GPU telemetry
# and prints them as JSON.
#
# This runs once when the widget loads, not on every tick. Sensor paths are
# stable for the life of a boot, so once they are known the widget samples by
# reading those files directly from inside the shell process — no subprocess
# per interval. Globbing and label matching is the one part that needs a
# shell, which is all this script is for.

...

# Package temperature. Every vendor names this differently, so score the
# candidates and keep the best: a die/package reading beats a per-core or
# per-CCD one, and any dedicated CPU sensor beats the ACPI thermal zone.
cpu_temp_path=""
cpu_temp_label=""
cpu_temp_score=0

consider_cpu_temp() {
 local score=$1 path=$2 label=$3
 ((score > cpu_temp_score)) || return 0
 [[ -r $path ]] || return 0
 cpu_temp_score=$score
 cpu_temp_path=$path
 cpu_temp_label=$label
}

for hwmon in /sys/class/hwmon/hwmon*; do
 name=$(read_first "$hwmon/name") || continue
 for input in "$hwmon"/temp*_input; do
 [[ -r $input ]] || continue
 label=$(read_first "${input%_input}_label" || true)
 case "$name" in
 k10temp | zenpower)
 case "$label" in
 Tdie) consider_cpu_temp 100 "$input" "${label:-$name}" ;;
 Tctl) consider_cpu_temp 95 "$input" "${label:-$name}" ;;
 *) consider_cpu_temp 60 "$input" "${label:-$name}" ;;
 esac
 ;;
 coretemp)
 case "$label" in
 "Package id"*) consider_cpu_temp 90 "$input" "$label" ;;
 *) consider_cpu_temp 55 "$input" "${label:-$name}" ;;
 esac
 ;;
 *thinkpad*)
 [[ $label == CPU ]] && consider_cpu_temp 65 "$input" "$label"
 ;;
 cpu_thermal | soc_thermal | scpi_sensors | cpu-thermal)
 consider_cpu_temp 70 "$input" "${label:-$name}"
 ;;
 acpitz)
 consider_cpu_temp 20 "$input" "${label:-$name}"
 ;;
 esac
 done
done

## [linux-kernel - [PATCH] hwmon: (coretemp) Document sysfs numbering and label meaning](https://lists.openwall.net/linux-kernel/2026/10/15/2727)
Published: 2026-10-15T00:00:00.000Z

```
Message-ID: <20261015171759.704526-1-ycistak@gmail.com>
Date: Thu, 15 Oct 2026 20:17:59 +0300
From: Asaf Nuri Bertizlioğlu <ycistak@...il.com>
To: linux@...ck-us.net,
	linux-hwmon@...r.kernel.org
Cc: corbet@....net,
	skhan@...uxfoundation.org,
	rdunlap@...radead.org,
	linux-doc@...r.kernel.org,
	linux-kernel@...r.kernel.org,
	Asaf Nuri Bertizlioğlu <ycistak@...il.com>
Subject: [PATCH] hwmon: (coretemp) Document sysfs numbering and label meaning

The documentation says that all sysfs entries are named with their
core_id, and that the "Core X" label contains the processor number.
Neither is accurate. create_core_attrs() numbers the per-core
attributes core_id + 2 and always uses temp1 for the package sensor,
and show_label() prints topology_core_id(), not the logical CPU
number.

The difference is easy to see on hybrid CPUs. On an i7-14700F, the
"Core 4" label is on temp6 and belongs to CPUs 2 and 3, and the
E-cores report core IDs 32 to 43.

Describe the actual numbering.

The mismatch was found by an LLM comparing the documentation with the
code, and verified on the i7-14700F above.

Assisted-by: LLM
Signed-off-by: Asaf Nuri Bertizlioğlu <ycistak@...il.com>
---

Notes:
    The LLM (Claude) also drafted the documentation change and this
    changelog. Values checked on my machine: temp6_label is "Core 4",
    cpu2 and cpu3 have core_id 4, temp1_label is "Package id 0".

 Documentation/hwmon/coretemp.rst | 12 ++++++++----
 1 file changed, 8 insertions(+), 4 deletions(-)

diff --git a/Documentation/hwmon/coretemp.rst b/Documentation/hwmon/coretemp.rst
index 34930168338..95b0a578c4d 100644
--- a/Documentation/hwmon/coretemp.rst
+++ b/Documentation/hwmon/coretemp.rst
@@ -46,7 +46,8 @@ mechanism will perform actions to forcibly cool down the processor. Alarm
 may be raised, if the temperature grows enough (more than TjMax) to trigger
 the Out-Of-Spec bit. The following table summarizes the exported sysfs files:
 
-All sysfs entries are named with their core_id (represented here by 'X').
+Per-core sysfs entries are numbered core_id + 2 (represented here by 'X').
+The package entries, if available, are always temp1_*.
 
 ================= ========================================================
 tempX_input	  Core temperature (in millidegrees Celsius).
@@ -55,9 +56,12 @@ tempX_max	  Maximum recommended operating temperature (Tcontrol).
 tempX_crit	  Maximum junction temperature (in millidegrees Celsius).
 tempX_crit_alarm  Set when Out-of-spec bit is set, never clears.
 		  Correct CPU operation is no longer guaranteed.
-tempX_label	  Contains string "Core X", where X is processor
-		  number. For Package temp, this will be "Package id Y",
-		  where Y is the package number.
+tempX_label	  Contains string "Core N", where N is the core_id
+		  (X - 2), as shown in
+		  /sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu*/topology/core_id. This
+		  is not the logical CPU number, and core_id values
+		  may be sparse. For Package temp, this will be
+		  "Package id Y", where Y is the package number.
 ================= ========================================================
 
 On modern CPUs (Nehalem and newer), TjMax is read from the
-- 
2.56.0

## [Linux-Kernel Archive: [PATCH] hwmon: (coretemp) Document sysfs numbering and label meaning](https://lkml.iu.edu/2610.1/19039.html)
Published: 2026-10-15T00:00:00.000Z

The documentation says that all sysfs entries are named with their core_id, and that the "Core X" label contains the processor number. Neither is accurate. create_core_attrs() numbers the per-core attributes core_id + 2 and always uses temp1 for the package sensor, and show_label() prints topology_core_id(), not the logical CPU number. The difference is easy to see on hybrid CPUs. On an i7-14700F, the "Core 4" label is on temp6 and belongs to CPUs 2 and 3, and the E-cores report core IDs 32 to 43. Describe the actual numbering.

---

## Assistant

_Thinking:_

The web search confirms:
- The `coretemp` kernel driver is Intel-only (DTS in Intel CPUs). On AMD, it's k10temp; on ARM/Grace, there's no coretemp.
- coretemp does create tempX_label files when the driver is present.

So my analysis stands. The answer to "Is my sampler_coretemp.conf correct?":

Yes, the config syntax is fine. The empty `core_list` means the plugin found zero coretemp sensors under `/sys/devices/platform` when the `config` command executed. The scan happens once at config time (nftw at coretemp.c:277) and only matches files of the form:

`/sys/devices/platform/coretemp.<pkg>/hwmon/hwmon<idx>/temp<no>_label`

(regex at coretemp.c:145-147). The sensor list is never re-scanned afterwards — `sample()` only refreshes current/max temps of the records created at config time.

So the possible causes:
1. No `coretemp.*` device at all → the `coretemp` kernel module isn't loaded, or the CPU doesn't use this driver at all:
   - Intel CPUs → `coretemp`
   - AMD EPYC → `k10temp` (lives at `/sys/devices/platform/k10temp.0/...`, the regex will never match it — this plugin can't read AMD CPU temps)
   - Grace/ARM (Alps GH200) → no coretemp driver; temps come from other hwmon drivers
2. `coretemp.*` exists but no `tempX_label` files → the regex requires `_label`. (Per kernel docs coretemp always creates labels, but on vendor kernels it could differ.)
3. ldmsd running in a container without `/sys/devices/platform` visible (common on Cray compute nodes where services run in PA/PALS containers).
4. Module loaded after ldmsd config ran → re-run config.

2. Explain where core_list comes from: built once at `config` time by walking /sys/devices/platform and matching regex (show regex + expected layout). sample() never re-scans.

5. What to do for each case; offer to patch the sampler if the labels are missing or the device is named differently.

## Short answer

Yes — the conf file itself is syntactically fine. The empty `core_list` is not caused by your `config` line; it means the plugin found **zero coretemp sensors on `/sys/devices/platform` at the moment `config` ran**. (`component_id` is optional, it defaults to 0 — you may want to add `component_id=6240` or whatever your canonical node id is, but that's unrelated to the empty list.)

## Why the list is empty

Looking at `coretemp.c`:

- The sensor tree is built **once, at `config` time**, by `nftw("/sys/devices/platform", create_sensors_cb, 16, FTW_PHYS)` (line 277).
- `create_sensors_cb()` only accepts regular files matching this regex (lines 145–147):

  ```
  /sys/devices/platform/coretemp.<pkg>/hwmon/hwmon<idx>/temp<no>_label
  ```

  i.e. a `tempX_label` file **must exist** — `tempX_input` alone is not enough.
- After that, the tree is never re-scanned: `sample()` only refreshes `current_temp`/`max_temp` on the records created at config time. If nothing matched at config time, the list stays `[]` forever.

So `core_list []` ⇔ no file matching `coretemp.*/hwmon/hwmon*/temp*_label` existed when the sampler was configured (the empty tree also gives `heap_sz = 0`, which matches exactly what you see).

## Things to check on nid006240

```bash
# 1. Does the coretemp hwmon device exist at all?
ls -d /sys/devices/platform/coretemp.* 2>&1

# 2. What would the sampler's regex actually match?
find /sys/devices/platform -name 'temp*_label' 2>/dev/null | grep coretemp

# 3. What hwmon devices does this node really expose?
for d in /sys/class/hwmon/hwmon*; do echo "$d: $(cat $d/name)"; done

# 4. Check the ldmsd log for "Error %d creating the sensor tree"
```

Depending on what you find:

1. **No `coretemp.*` directory at all** — the `coretemp` kernel module isn't loaded, or the CPU doesn't use this driver. Note this driver is **Intel-only**:
   - Intel → `modprobe coretemp` fixes it.
   - AMD EPYC → CPU temps come from `k10temp` (`/sys/devices/platform/k10temp.0/hwmon/hwmonN/...`), which this regex will **never** match — the coretemp sampler can't read AMD CPU temps at all.
   - Grace/ARM-based nodes (GH200-style) have no `coretemp` driver either; temps are exported by other hwmon devices (see what step 3 lists).
2. **`coretemp.*` exists but no `temp*_label` files** → nothing matches the regex; the plugin would need a small patch to make `_label` optional (it currently keys discovery off the label file).
3. **The files exist but ldmsd still sees nothing** — check whether ldmsd runs inside a container/namespace on the compute node that doesn't have `/sys/devices/platform` mounted, and whether `coretemp` was loaded *after* the `config` command ran (in that case just `term name=coretemp` and re-`load`/`config`, or restart the sampler service — the scan is not retried).

If it turns out the node exposes temps under a different device name (e.g. `k10temp`) or without `_label` files, I can help you patch the regex/discovery logic in `coretemp.c` to handle that case — let me know what steps 1–3 print.

That settles it — your conf was fine, but the plugin is being asked to monitor hardware that doesn't exist on this node.

## Root cause: no coretemp device on this node

- `/sys/devices/platform/coretemp.*` → doesn't exist
- `temp*_label` under `/sys/devices/platform` → none
- hwmon devices are only `cxi0`–`cxi3` (Cassini/Slingshot NIC drivers) and `acpitz` (ACPI thermal zone)

The tree also shows this is an **ARM / NVIDIA Grace Hopper (GH200) node**: `ARMH0011:00` (sbsa-uart), `arm-smmu-v3.*`, `smccc_trng`, `NVDA1513:00`, `sdei`, and the `ACPI000D:*` power meters.

The `coretemp` kernel driver is **Intel x86-only** — it reads the CPU's Digital Temperature Sensor via x86 MSRs, so it can never appear on a Grace CPU. The plugin's regex (`coretemp.<pkg>/hwmon/hwmon<idx>/temp<n>_label`) can therefore never match anything here, the sensor tree is empty at `config` time, and `core_list` stays `[]` forever. No change to `sampler_coretemp.conf` can fix this — it's the wrong plugin for this architecture. (You can drop the sampler, or leave it; it's harmless but useless here.)

## What temperature data does exist on the node

Only the ACPI thermal zone right now. To see exactly what's readable:

```bash
ls -l /sys/class/hwmon/hwmon4/
cat /sys/class/hwmon/hwmon4/temp*_input 2>/dev/null
for z in /sys/class/thermal/thermal_zone*; do
    echo "$z: $(cat $z/type) = $(cat $z/temp) millidegC"
done
sensors   # if lm-sensors is installed, same picture
```

## Options going forward

1. **A single zone temp is enough** → LDMS's `filesingle` sampler can read one sysfs file (e.g. `/sys/class/thermal/thermal_zone0/temp`) into a set, no code needed. Clunky if there are several zones.
2. **You want proper per-zone temperature metrics** → there's no generic hwmon/thermal sampler in this LDMS tree (I checked — the closest ARM thing is `tx2mon`, which is Marvell-specific). I can write you a small `acpitz`/`hwmon` sampler in the same style as `coretemp.c` (walk `/sys/class/thermal`, one record per zone with type/temp/trip points). Given all Grace nodes are identical, the schema would be uniform across the cluster.
3. **GPU temps on GH200** → the `dcgm_sampler` (already in this tree) covers the Hopper side.
4. **Out-of-band** → on Alps systems node environmental data (including CPU temps) is usually also available from the BMC via Redfish, which some sites prefer for alerting anyway.

Run the thermal-zone commands above and tell me what they print — if you want option 2, paste the output and I'll draft the sampler against it.

scandir numeric sort:

```c
static int zone_cmp(const struct dirent **a, const struct dirent **b)
{
    int na = atoi((*a)->d_name + strlen("thermal_zone"));
    int nb = atoi((*b)->d_name + strlen("thermal_zone"));
    return na - nb;
}
```

Filter:

```c
static int zone_filter(const struct dirent *d)
{
    const char *p = d->d_name;
    if (strncmp(p, "thermal_zone", 12)) return 0;
    p += 12;
    if (!*p) return 0;
    while (*p) { if (!isdigit((unsigned char)*p)) return 0; p++; }
    return 1;
}
```

Reading helper:

```c
static int read_int_file(const char *path, long *out)
{
    FILE *fp = fopen(path, "r");
    if (!fp) return -1;
    char buf[64];
    int rc = fread(buf, 1, sizeof(buf) - 1, fp);
    fclose(fp);
    if (rc <= 0) return -1;
    buf[rc] = '\0';
    *out = strtol(buf, NULL, 0);
    return 0;
}
```

Hmm, careful: fread could return sizeof(buf)-1 if file content longer; buf[rc] = 0 fine.

Type reading:

```c
static int read_type(const char *path, char *buf, size_t buf_sz)
{
    FILE *fp = fopen(path, "r");
    if (!fp) return -1;
    size_t rc = fread(buf, 1, buf_sz - 1, fp);
    fclose(fp);
    if (rc <= 0) return -1;
    buf[rc] = '\0';
    char *nl = strchr(buf, '\n');
    if (nl) *nl = '\0';
    return 0;
}
```

```c
#define _GNU_SOURCE
#include <ctype.h>
#include <dirent.h>
#include <inttypes.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <stdio.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <sys/errno.h>
#include <sys/types.h>
#include <linux/limits.h>
#include "../sampler_base.h"
#include "ldms.h"
#include "ldmsd.h"
#include "ldmsd_plug_api.h"

#define SAMP "thermal_zones"
#define ZONE_NAME_PREFIX "thermal_zone"
#define ZONE_TYPE_SZ 32
#define MAX_TRIP_POINTS 16

struct zone {
	int zone_no;		/* N in thermal_zoneN */
	char type[ZONE_TYPE_SZ]; /* thermal_zoneN/type */
	char *temp_path;
	FILE *temp_fp;
	float temp;		/* thermal_zoneN/temp, C */
	float trip_temp;	/* highest trip point temp, C; -1.0 if none */
	ldms_mval_t zone_v;	/* this zone's record value */
};

static struct zone *zones;
static int zone_count;
static int zone_list_idx;
static int zone_rec_idx;
static ldms_set_t set;
static ovis_log_t mylog;
static base_data_t base;

struct ldms_metric_template_s zone_template[] = {
	{ "zone_no", LDMS_MDESC_F_DATA, LDMS_V_U32, "", 1 },
	{ "type", LDMS_MDESC_F_DATA, LDMS_V_CHAR_ARRAY, "", ZONE_TYPE_SZ },
	{ "temp", LDMS_MDESC_F_DATA, LDMS_V_F32, "C", 1 },
	{ "trip_temp", LDMS_MDESC_F_DATA, LDMS_V_F32, "C", 1 },
	{}
};
static int zone_metric_ids[sizeof(zone_template) / sizeof(zone_template[0])];
#define ZONE_NO_IDX	0
#define TYPE_IDX	1
#define TEMP_IDX	2
#define TRIP_TEMP_IDX	3
```

Helper functions:

```c
static int read_line(const char *path, char *buf, size_t buf_sz)
{
	FILE *fp;
	size_t rc;
	char *nl;

	fp = fopen(path, "r");
	if (!fp)
		return -1;
	rc = fread(buf, 1, buf_sz - 1, fp);
	fclose(fp);
	if (rc == 0)
		return -1;
	buf[rc] = '\0';
	nl = strchr(buf, '\n');
	if (nl)
		*nl = '\0';
	return 0;
}
```

For temp: `long v; read_line -> strtol`.

scandir filter + sort:

```c
static int zone_filter(const struct dirent *d)
{
	const char *p = d->d_name;
	if (strncmp(p, ZONE_NAME_PREFIX, sizeof(ZONE_NAME_PREFIX) - 1))
		return 0;
	p += sizeof(ZONE_NAME_PREFIX) - 1;
	if (!*p)
		return 0;
	for (; *p; p++)
		if (!isdigit(*p))
			return 0;
	return 1;
}

static int zone_cmp(const struct dirent **a, const struct dirent **b)
{
	int na = atoi((*a)->d_name + sizeof(ZONE_NAME_PREFIX) - 1);
	int nb = atoi((*b)->d_name + sizeof(ZONE_NAME_PREFIX) - 1);
	return na - nb;
}
```

discover_zones():

```c
static int discover_zones(void)
{
	int rc, i;
	struct dirent **namelist;
	char path[PATH_MAX];
	long temp, trip;
	int n;

	n = scandir("/sys/class/thermal", &namelist, zone_filter, zone_cmp);
	if (n < 0)
		return errno;
	if (n == 0)
		return ENOENT;

	zones = calloc(n, sizeof(*zones));
	if (!zones) {
		rc = ENOMEM;
		goto out;
	}
	zone_count = n;

	for (i = 0; i < n; i++) {
		struct zone *z = &zones[i];
		z->zone_no = atoi(namelist[i]->d_name + sizeof(ZONE_NAME_PREFIX) - 1);
		z->trip_temp = -1.0;
		z->temp = 0.0;
		rc = snprintf(path, sizeof(path), "/sys/class/thermal/%s/temp",
				namelist[i]->d_name);
		z->temp_path = strdup(path);
		rc = snprintf(path, sizeof(path), "/sys/class/thermal/%s/type",
				namelist[i]->d_name);
		if (read_line(path, z->type, sizeof(z->type)))
			strncpy(z->type, "unknown", sizeof(z->type) - 1); // hmm
		/* Highest trip point, if any */
		int j;
		for (j = 0; j < MAX_TRIP_POINTS; j++) {
			char tpath[PATH_MAX];
			snprintf(tpath, sizeof(tpath), "/sys/class/thermal/%s/trip_point_%d_temp",
				 namelist[i]->d_name, j);
			if (read_long(tpath, &trip))
				break;
			if (trip > z->trip_temp)
				z->trip_temp = trip / 1000.0;
		}
	}
out:
	for (i = 0; i < n; i++)
		free(namelist[i]);
	free(namelist);
	return rc;
}
```

Careful with rc handling — I'll restructure cleanly.

read_long helper:

```c
static int read_long(const char *path, long *out)
{
	char buf[64];
	if (read_line(path, buf, sizeof(buf)))
		return -1;
	*out = strtol(buf, NULL, 0);
	return 0;
}
```

create_metric_set:

```c
static int create_metric_set(base_data_t base)
{
	int rc;
	ldms_schema_t schema;
	int i;

	rc = discover_zones();
	if (rc) {
		ovis_log(mylog, OVIS_LERROR,
			"No thermal zones found under /sys/class/thermal, errno=%d.\n", rc);
		goto err;
	}

	schema = base_schema_new(base);
	if (!schema) { ... }

	ldms_record_t zone_def =
		ldms_record_from_template("zone_temps", zone_template, zone_metric_ids);

	zone_rec_idx = ldms_schema_record_add(schema, zone_def);
	if (zone_rec_idx < 0) { rc = -zone_rec_idx; goto err; }

	size_t heap_sz = zone_count * ldms_record_heap_size_get(zone_def);
	zone_list_idx = ldms_schema_metric_list_add(schema, "zone_list", NULL, heap_sz);
	if (zone_list_idx < 0) { rc = -zone_list_idx; goto err; }

	set = base_set_new(base);
	if (!set) { rc = errno; ovis_log(...); goto err; }

	ldms_mval_t zone_list_v = ldms_metric_get(set, zone_list_idx);
	for (i = 0; i < zone_count; i++) {
		struct zone *z = &zones[i];
		long t;
		if (!read_long(z->temp_path, &t))
			z->temp = t / 1000.0;
		z->zone_v = ldms_record_alloc(set, zone_rec_idx);
		ldms_record_set_u32(z->zone_v, ZONE_NO_IDX, z->zone_no);
		ldms_record_array_set_str(z->zone_v, TYPE_IDX, z->type);
		ldms_record_set_float(z->zone_v, TEMP_IDX, z->temp);
		ldms_record_set_float(z->zone_v, TRIP_TEMP_IDX, z->trip_temp);
		rc = ldms_list_append_record(set, zone_list_v, z->zone_v);
		if (rc) { ovis_log(...); goto err; }
	}
	return 0;
err:
	return rc;
}
```

static int sample(ldmsd_plug_handle_t handle)
{
	int i;

	if (!set) {
		ovis_log(mylog, OVIS_LDEBUG, "plugin not initialized\n");
		return EINVAL;
	}

	base_sample_begin(base);

	for (i = 0; i < zone_count; i++) {
		struct zone *z = &zones[i];
		long t;
		if (!z->temp_fp)
			z->temp_fp = fopen(z->temp_path, "r");
		else
			fseek(z->temp_fp, 0, SEEK_SET);
		if (!z->temp_fp || fread(&t...)) 
		...
		ldms_record_set_float(z->zone_v, TEMP_IDX, z->temp);
	}

	base_sample_end(base);
	return 0;
}
```

Use read via fp: char buf[64]; rc = fread(buf,1,sizeof(buf)-1, fp); if rc>0 { buf[rc]=0; z->temp = strtol(buf,NULL,0)/1000.0; }

If open/read fails: keep previous value, log at OVIS_LDEBUG (avoid log spam every 10s at error level).

destructor:

```c
static void destructor(ldmsd_plug_handle_t handle)
{
	int i;
	for (i = 0; i < zone_count; i++) {
		free(zones[i].temp_path);
		if (zones[i].temp_fp)
			fclose(zones[i].temp_fp);
	}
	free(zones);
	zones = NULL;
	zone_count = 0;
	if (base)
		base_del(base);
	if (set)
		ldms_set_delete(set);
	set = NULL;
}
```

config():

```c
static int config(ldmsd_plug_handle_t handle, struct attr_value_list *kwl, struct attr_value_list *avl)
{
	int rc;

	if (set) {
		ovis_log(mylog, OVIS_LERROR, "Set already created.\n");
		return EINVAL;
	}

	base = base_config(avl, ldmsd_plug_cfg_name_get(handle), SAMP, mylog);
	if (!base) {
		rc = EINVAL;
		goto err;
	}

	rc = create_metric_set(base);
	if (rc) {
		ovis_log(mylog, OVIS_LERROR, "failed to create a metric set.\n");
		goto err;
	}
	return 0;
err:
	base_del(base);
	base = NULL;
	return rc;
}
```

#define _GNU_SOURCE
#include <ctype.h>
#include <dirent.h>
#include <inttypes.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <stdio.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <sys/errno.h>
#include <sys/types.h>
#include <linux/limits.h>
#include "../sampler_base.h"
#include "ldms.h"
#include "ldmsd.h"
#include "ldmsd_plug_api.h"

#define SAMP "thermal_zones"
#define ZONE_PREFIX "thermal_zone"
#define ZONE_TYPE_SZ 32
#define MAX_TRIP_POINTS 16

/*
 * One Linux thermal zone, i.e. /sys/class/thermal/thermal_zone<N>. The
 * zones are discovered once, when the 'config' action runs, and each
 * zone is represented by a record in the 'zone_list' list metric.
 */
struct zone {
	int zone_no;		/* N in thermal_zoneN */
	char type[ZONE_TYPE_SZ]; /* content of thermal_zoneN/type */
	char *temp_path;	/* thermal_zoneN/temp */
	FILE *temp_fp;
	float temp;		/* thermal_zoneN/temp in C */
	float trip_temp;	/* highest trip point temp in C, -1 if none */
	ldms_mval_t zone_v;	/* this zone's record value */
};

struct ldms_metric_template_s zone_template[] = {
	{ "zone_no", LDMS_MDESC_F_DATA, LDMS_V_U32, "", 1 },
	{ "type", LDMS_MDESC_F_DATA, LDMS_V_CHAR_ARRAY, "", ZONE_TYPE_SZ },
	{ "temp", LDMS_MDESC_F_DATA, LDMS_V_F32, "C", 1 },
	{ "trip_temp", LDMS_MDESC_F_DATA, LDMS_V_F32, "C", 1 },
	{}
};
static int zone_metric_ids[sizeof(zone_template) / sizeof(zone_template[0])];
#define ZONE_NO_IDX	0
#define TYPE_IDX	1
#define TEMP_IDX	2
#define TRIP_TEMP_IDX	3

static struct zone *zones;
static int zone_count;
static int zone_list_idx;
static int zone_rec_idx;
static ldms_set_t set;
#define SAMP ...
static ovis_log_t mylog;
static base_data_t base;
```

Helper read functions:

```c
static int read_line(const char *path, char *buf, size_t buf_sz)
{
	FILE *fp;
	size_t rc;
	char *nl;

	fp = fopen(path, "r");
	if (!fp)
		return -1;
	rc = fread(buf, 1, buf_sz - 1, fp);
	fclose(fp);
	if (rc == 0)
		return -1;
	buf[rc] = '\0';
	nl = strchr(buf, '\n');
	if (nl)
		*nl = '\0';
	return 0;
}

static int read_long(const char *path, long *out)
{
	char buf[64];

	if (read_line(path, buf, sizeof(buf)))
		return -1;
	*out = strtol(buf, NULL, 0);
	return 0;
}
```

Filter and sort:

```c
static int zone_filter(const struct dirent *d)
{
	const char *p = d->d_name;

	if (strncmp(p, ZONE_PREFIX, sizeof(ZONE_PREFIX) - 1))
		return 0;
	p += sizeof(ZONE_PREFIX) - 1;
	if (!*p)
		return 0;
	for (; *p != '\0'; p++) {
		if (!isdigit(*p))
			return 0;
	}
	return 1;
}

static int zone_sort(const struct dirent **a, const struct dirent **b)
{
	int na = atoi((*a)->d_name + sizeof(ZONE_PREFIX) - 1);
	int nb = atoi((*b)->d_name + sizeof(ZONE_PREFIX) - 1);
	return na - nb;
}
```

Note: `sizeof(ZONE_PREFIX) - 1` where ZONE_PREFIX is a string literal → strlen. Good.

discover_zones:

```c
static int discover_zones(void)
{
	struct dirent **namelist = NULL;
	char path[PATH_MAX];
	int n, i, j;
	int rc = 0;
	long v;

	n = scandir("/sys/class/thermal", &namelist, zone_filter, zone_sort);
	if (n < 0)
		return errno;
	if (n == 0) {
		rc = ENOENT;
		goto out;
	}

	zones = calloc(n, sizeof(*zones));
	if (!zones) {
		rc = ENOMEM;
		goto out;
	}
	zone_count = n;

	for (i = 0; i < n; i++) {
		struct zone *z = &zones[i];

		z->zone_no = atoi(namelist[i]->d_name + sizeof(ZONE_PREFIX) - 1);
		z->trip_temp = -1.0;

		snprintf(path, sizeof(path), "/sys/class/thermal/%s/temp",
			 namelist[i]->d_name);
		z->temp_path = strdup(path);

		snprintf(path, sizeof(path), "/sys/class/thermal/%s/type",
			 namelist[i]->d_name);
		if (read_line(path, z->type, sizeof(z->type)))
			snprintf(z->type, sizeof(z->type), "unknown");

		/* The highest trip point temperature, if the zone has any. */
		for (j = 0; j < MAX_TRIP_POINTS; j++) {
			snprintf(path, sizeof(path),
				 "/sys/class/thermal/%s/trip_point_%d_temp",
				 namelist[i]->d_name, j);
			if (read_long(path, &v))
				break;
			if (v / 1000.0 > z->trip_temp)
				z->trip_temp = v / 1000.0;
		}
	}
 out:
	if (namelist) {
		for (i = 0; i < n; i++)
			free(namelist[i]);
		free(namelist);
	}
	return rc;
}
```

create_metric_set:

```c
static int create_metric_set(base_data_t base)
{
	int rc;
	ldms_schema_t schema;
	int i;

	rc = discover_zones();
	if (rc) {
		ovis_log(mylog, OVIS_LERROR,
			 "No thermal zones found under /sys/class/thermal, errno=%d.\n",
			 rc);
		goto err;
	}

	/* Create a metric set of the required size */
	schema = base_schema_new(base);
	if (!schema) {
		ovis_log(mylog, OVIS_LERROR,
			 "The schema '%s' could not be created, errno=%d.\n",
			 base->schema_name, errno);
		rc = EINVAL;
		goto err;
	}

	ldms_record_t zone_def =
		ldms_record_from_template("zone_temps",
					  zone_template, zone_metric_ids);

	/* Add record definition into the schema */
	zone_rec_idx = ldms_schema_record_add(schema, zone_def);
	if (zone_rec_idx < 0) {
		rc = -zone_rec_idx;
		goto err;
	}

	/* Add a list of thermal zones */
	rc = ldms_schema_metric_list_add(schema, "zone_list", NULL,
					 zone_count * ldms_record_heap_size_get(zone_def));
	...
```

Hmm wait — careful: `ldms_record_from_template` returns a `ldms_record_t`; `ldms_record_heap_size_get(zone_def)` — used in coretemp before schema_record_add. Order in coretemp: heap_sz computed after record_from_template, before schema_record_add. Same here.

But careful: I assign `zone_list_idx = ldms_schema_metric_list_add(...)`; need the int. Write:

```c
	zone_list_idx = ldms_schema_metric_list_add(schema, "zone_list", NULL,
			zone_count * ldms_record_heap_size_get(zone_def));
	if (zone_list_idx < 0) {
		rc = -zone_list_idx;
		goto err;
	}

	set = base_set_new(base);
	if (!set) {
		ovis_log(mylog, OVIS_LERROR, "Error %d creating the metric set.\n", errno);
		rc = errno;
		goto err;
	}

	/* Populate the list with a record for each thermal zone */
	ldms_mval_t zone_list_v;
	zone_list_v = ldms_metric_get(set, zone_list_idx);
	for (i = 0; i < zone_count; i++) {
		struct zone *z = &zones[i];
		long v;

		if (!read_long(z->temp_path, &v))
			z->temp = v / 1000.0;

		z->zone_v = ldms_record_alloc(set, zone_rec_idx);
		ldms_record_set_u32(z->zone_v, ZONE_NO_IDX, z->zone_no);
		ldms_record_array_set_str(z->zone_v, TYPE_IDX, z->type);
		ldms_record_set_float(z->zone_v, TEMP_IDX, z->temp);
		ldms_record_set_float(z->zone_v, TRIP_TEMP_IDX, z->trip_temp);
		rc = ldms_list_append_record(set, zone_list_v, z->zone_v);
		if (rc) {
			ovis_log(mylog, OVIS_LERROR,
				 "Failed to append record for zone %d.\n", z->zone_no);
			goto err;
		}
	}
 err:
	return rc;
}
```

usage/config/sample/constructor/destructor:

```c
static const char *usage(ldmsd_plug_handle_t handle)
{
	return "config name=" SAMP BASE_CONFIG_USAGE;
}

static int config(ldmsd_plug_handle_t handle, struct attr_value_list *kwl, struct attr_value_list *avl)
{
	int rc;

	if (set) {
		ovis_log(mylog, OVIS_LERROR, "Set already created.\n");
		return EINVAL;
	}

	base = base_config(avl, ldmsd_plug_cfg_name_get(handle), SAMP, mylog);
	if (!base) {
		rc = EINVAL;
		goto err;
	}

	rc = create_metric_set(base);
	if (rc) {
		ovis_log(mylog, OVIS_LERROR, "failed to create a metric set.\n");
		goto err;
	}

	return 0;

 err:
	base_del(base);
	base = NULL;
	return rc;
}

static int sample(ldmsd_plug_handle_t handle)
{
	int i;

	if (!set) {
		ovis_log(mylog, OVIS_LDEBUG, "plugin not initialized\n");
		return EINVAL;
	}

	base_sample_begin(base);

	/* Update the zone temperatures */
	for (i = 0; i < zone_count; i++) {
		struct zone *z = &zones[i];
		char fbuf[64];
		size_t rc;

		if (!z->temp_fp)
			z->temp_fp = fopen(z->temp_path, "r");
		else
			fseek(z->temp_fp, 0, SEEK_SET);
		if (!z->temp_fp)
			continue;
		rc = fread(fbuf, 1, sizeof(fbuf) - 1, z->temp_fp);
		if (rc == 0)
			continue;
		fbuf[rc] = '\0';
		z->temp = strtol(fbuf, NULL, 0) / 1000.0;
		ldms_record_set_float(z->zone_v, TEMP_IDX, z->temp);
	}

	base_sample_end(base);
	return 0;
}

static int constructor(ldmsd_plug_handle_t handle)
{
	mylog = ldmsd_plug_log_get(handle);
	set = NULL;

	return 0;
}

static void destructor(ldmsd_plug_handle_t handle)
{
	int i;

	for (i = 0; i < zone_count; i++) {
		free(zones[i].temp_path);
		if (zones[i].temp_fp)
			fclose(zones[i].temp_fp);
	}
	free(zones);
	zones = NULL;
	zone_count = 0;
	if (base)
		base_del(base);
	base = NULL;
	if (set)
		ldms_set_delete(set);
	set = NULL;
}

struct ldmsd_sampler ldmsd_plugin_interface = {
	.base = {
		.type = LDMSD_PLUGIN_SAMPLER,
		.config = config,
		.usage = usage,
		.constructor = constructor,
		.destructor = destructor,
	},
	.sample = sample,
};
```

Wait — one issue in `sample()`: `fread(fbuf, 1, sizeof(fbuf)-1, fp)` — on this kernel the sysfs files report size 65536 but fread stops at EOF of actual content (read returns the number string). fread with size 63 will do a single read() syscall getting e.g. "39100\n" (6 bytes) — rc=6. Good. Actually careful: fread loops until 63 bytes or EOF. read() returns 6, next read returns 0 → fread returns 6. OK.

But — hmm, there's a subtlety with fseek on sysfs files: seeking to 0 and reading works (sysfs regenerates content on read). Coretemp does the same. Good.

Another subtlety: `ldms_record_set_float(z->zone_v, TRIP_TEMP_IDX, z->trip_temp)` — set once at config; fine.

`struct attr_value_list *kwl` unused → may produce unused-parameter warning; coretemp has same signature, fine.

`read_line` for `type`: file "acpitz\n" → buf "acpitz". Good.

One more check — `ldms_record_from_template("zone_temps", ...)`: the record type name. In ldms_ls output this shows as `record_type zone_temps`. Good, mirrors core_temps.

Also — is `ldms_record_alloc` + `ldms_list_append_record` rc semantics: append returns 0 on success? In coretemp they ignore. `ldms_list_append_record` returns 0 or -errno (int). Checking `if (rc)` is right, but note rc is reused from earlier success path... after the loop rc would be 0 from append. At `err:` we return rc. Fine.

Edge: `zone_metric_ids` array size — template has 5 entries (4 + terminator) → ids array size 5. `ldms_record_from_template` fills ids. ✓ same as coretemp.

rst doc — modeled on coretemp's:

```rst
.. _thermal_zones:

===============
thermal_zones
===============

-------------------------------------------------------------------
An LDMS sampler plugin that monitors Linux thermal zone temperatures
-------------------------------------------------------------------

:Date:   November 2026
:Manual section: 7
:Manual group: LDMS sampler


SYNOPSIS
========

| Within ldmsd_controller or a configuration file:
| load name=thermal_zones config name=thermal_zones producer=<name>
  instance=<name> component_id=<int>

DESCRIPTION
===========

The thermal_zones sampler collects temperatures from the Linux thermal
zone interface at /sys/class/thermal. Directories named thermal_zone<N>
in this directory are enumerated, and each zone is represented by a
record in the zone_list list metric containing:

- zone_no: the zone number N
- type: the content of thermal_zone<N>/type (e.g. acpitz)
- temp: the zone temperature in Celsius (thermal_zone<N>/temp)
- trip_temp: the highest trip point temperature in Celsius
  (thermal_zone<N>/trip_point_*_temp), or -1 if the zone has none

Zones are discovered once when the config action runs. Zones that
appear or disappear afterwards are not detected until the sampler is
re-configured.

CONFIGURATION ATTRIBUTE SYNTAX
==============================

See man base.

EXAMPLES
========

Within ldmsd_controller or a configuration file:

::

   load name=thermal_zones
   config name=thermal_zones producer=vm1_1 instance=vm1_1/thermal_zones
   start name=thermal_zones interval=1000000 offset=0

SEE ALSO
========

:ref:`ldmsd(8) <ldmsd>`, :ref:`ldms_quickstart(7) <ldms_quickstart>`, :ref:`ldmsd_controller(8) <ldmsd_controller>`, :ref:`ldms_sampler_base(7) <ldms_sampler_base>`,
:ref:`coretemp(7) <coretemp>`
```

*p != '\\0'; p++) {\n\t\tif (!isdigit(*p))\n\t\t\treturn 0;\n\t}\n\treturn 1;\n}\n\n/* Sort zones numerically: thermal_zone2 before thermal_zone10 */\nstatic int zone_sort(const struct dirent **a, const struct dirent **b)\n{\n\tint na = atoi((*a)->d_name + sizeof(ZONE_PREFIX) - 1);\n\tint nb = atoi((*b)->d_name + sizeof(ZONE_PREFIX) - 1);\n\treturn na - nb;\n}\n\nstatic int discover_zones(void)\n{\n\tstruct dirent **namelist = NULL;\n\tchar path[PATH_MAX];\n\tint n, i, j;\n\tint rc = 0;\n\tlong v;\n\n\tn = scandir(\"/sys/class/thermal\", &namelist, zone_filter, zone_sort);\n\tif (n < 0)\n\t\treturn errno;\n\tif (n == 0) {\n\t\trc = ENOENT;\n\t\tgoto out;\n\t}\n\n\tzones = calloc(n, sizeof(*zones));\n\tif (!zones) {\n\t\trc = ENOMEM;\n\t\tgoto out;\n\t}\n\tzone_count = n;\n\n\tfor (i = 0; i < n; i++) {\n\t\tstruct zone *z = &zones[i];\n\n\t\tz->zone_no = atoi(namelist[i]->d_name + sizeof(ZONE_PREFIX) - 1);\n\t\tz->trip_temp = -1.0;\n\n\t\tsnprintf(path, sizeof(path), \"/sys/class/thermal/%s/temp\",\n\t\t\t namelist[i]->d_name);\n\t\tz->temp_path = strdup(path);\n\n\t\tsnprintf(path, sizeof(path), \"/sys/class/thermal/%s/type\",\n\t\t\t namelist[i]->d_name);\n\t\tif (read_line(path, z->type, sizeof(z->type)))\n\t\t\tsnprintf(z->type, sizeof(z->type), \"unknown\");\n\n\t\t/* The highest trip point temperature, if the zone has any. */\n\t\tfor (j = 0; i < n; i++)\n\t\t\tfree(namelist[i]);\n\t\tfree(namelist);\n\t}\n\treturn rc;\n}\n\nstatic int create_metric_set(base_data_t base)\n{\n\tint rc;\n\tldms_schema_t schema;\n\tint i;\n\n\trc = discover_zones();\n\tif (rc) {\n\t\tovis_log(mylog, OVIS_LERROR,\n\t\t\t \"No thermal zones found under /sys/class/thermal, errno=%d.\\n\",\n\t\t\t rc);\n\t\tgoto err;\n\t}\n\n\t/* Create a metric set of the required size */\n\tschema = base_schema_new(base);\n\tif (!schema) {\n\t\tovis_log(mylog, OVIS_LERROR,\n\t\t       \"The schema '%s' could not be created, errno=%d.\\n\",\n\t\t       base->schema_name, errno);\n\t\trc = EINVAL;\n\t\tgoto err;\n\t}\n\n\tldms_record_t zone_def =\n\t\tldms_record_from_template(\"zone_temps\",\n\t\t\t\t\t  zone_template, zone_metric_ids);\n\n\t/* Add record definition into the schema */\n\tzone_rec_idx = ldms_schema_record_add(schema, zone_def);\n\tif (zone_rec_idx < 0) {\n\t\trc = -zone_rec_idx;\n\t\tgoto err;\n\t}\n\n\t/* Add a list of thermal zones */\n\tzone_list_idx = ldms_schema_metric_list_add(schema, \"zone_list\", NULL,\n\t\t\tzone_count * ldms_record_heap_size_get(zone_def));\n\tif (zone_list_idx < 0) {\n\t\trc = -zone_list_idx;\n\t\tgoto err;\n\t}\n\n\tset = base_set_new(base);\n\tif (!set) {\n\t\tovis_log(mylog, OVIS_LERROR, \"Error %d creating the metric set.\\n\", errno);\n\t\trc = errno;\n\t\tgoto err;\n\t}\n\n\t/* Populate the list with a record for each thermal zone */\n\tldms_mval_t zone_list_v;\n\tzone_list_v = ldms_metric_get(set, zone_list_idx);\n\tfor (i = 0; i < zone_count; i++) {\n\t\tstruct zone *z = &zones[i];\n\t\tlong v;\n\n\t\tif (!read_long(z->temp_path, &v))\n\t\t\tz->temp = v / 1000.0;\n\n\t\tz->zone_v = ldms_record_alloc(set, zone_rec_idx);\n\t\tldms_record_set_u32(z->zone_v, ZONE_NO_IDX, z->zone_no);\n\t\tldms_record_array_set_str(z->zone_v, TYPE_IDX, z->type);\n\t\tldms_record_set_float(z->zone_v, TEMP_IDX, z->temp);\n\t\tldms_record_set_float(z->zone_v, TRIP_TEMP_IDX, z->trip_temp);\n\t\trc = ldms_list_append_record(set, zone_list_v, z->zone_v);\n\t\tif (rc) {\n\t\t\tovis_log(mylog, OVIS_LERROR,\n\t\t\t\t \"Failed to append the record for thermal_zone%d.\\n\",\n\t\t\t\t z->zone_no);\n\t\t\tgoto err;\n\t\t}\n\t}\n err:\n\treturn rc;\n}\n\n\nstatic const char *usage(ldmsd_plug_handle_t handle)\n{\n\treturn \"config name=\" SAMP BASE_CONFIG_USAGE;\n}\n\nstatic int config(ldmsd_plug_handle_t handle, struct attr_value_list *kwl, struct attr_value_list *avl)\n{\n\tint rc;\n\n\tif (set) {\n\t\tovis_log(mylog, OVIS_LERROR, \"Set already created.\\n\");\n\t\treturn EINVAL;\n\t}\n\n\tbase = base_config(avl, ldmsd_plug_cfg_name_get(handle), SAMP, mylog);\n\tif (!base) {\n\t\trc = EINVAL;\n\t\tgoto err;\n\t}\n\n\trc = create_metric_set(base);\n\tif (rc) {\n\t\tovis_log(mylog, OVIS_LERROR, \"failed to create a metric set.\\n\");\n\t\tgoto err;\n\t}\n\n\treturn 0;\n\n err:\n\tbase_del(base);\n\tbase = NULL;\n\treturn rc;\n\n}\n\nstatic int sample(ldmsd_plug_handle_t handle)\n{\n\tint i;\n\n\tif (!set){\n\t\tovis_log(mylog, OVIS_LDEBUG, \"plugin not initialized\\n\");\n\t\treturn EINVAL;\n\t}\n\n\tbase_sample_begin(base);\n\n\t/* Update the zone temperatures */\n\tfor (i = 0; i < zone_count; i++) {\n\t\tstruct zone *z = &zones[i];\n\t\tchar fbuf[64];\n\t\tsize_t rc;\n\n\t\tif (!z->temp_fp)\n\t\t\tz->temp_fp = fopen(z->temp_path, \"r\");\n\t\telse\n\t\t\tfseek(z->temp_fp, 0, SEEK_SET);\n\t\tif (!z->temp_fp)\n\t\t\tcontinue;\n\t\trc = fread(fbuf, 1, sizeof(fbuf) - 1, z->temp_fp);\n\t\tif (rc == 0)\n\t\t\tcontinue;\n\t\tfbuf[rc] = '\\0';\n\t\tz->temp = strtol(fbuf, NULL, 0) / 1000.0;\n\t\tldms_record_set_float(z->zone_v, TEMP_IDX, z->temp);\n\t}\n\n\tbase_sample_end(base);\n\treturn 0;\n}\n\nstatic int constructor(ldmsd_plug_handle_t handle)\n{\n\tmylog = ldmsd_plug_log_get(handle);\n\tset = NULL;\n\n        return 0;\n}\n\nstatic void destructor(ldmsd_plug_handle_t handle)\n{\n\tint i;\n\n\t/* Clean up the zones */\n\tfor (i = 0; Zones that\nappear or disappear afterwards are not detected until the sampler is\nre-configured.\n\nCONFIGURATION ATTRIBUTE SYNTAX\n==============================\n\nSee man base.\n\nEXAMPLES\n========\n\nWithin ldmsd_controller or a configuration file:\n\n::\n\n   load name=thermal_zones\n   config name=thermal_zones producer=vm1_1 instance=vm1_1/thermal_zones\n   start name=thermal_zones interval=1000000 offset=0\n\nSEE ALSO\n========\n\n:ref:`ldmsd(8) <ldmsd>`, :ref:`ldms_quickstart(7) <ldms_quickstart>`, :ref:`ldmsd_controller(8) <ldmsd_controller>`, :ref:`ldms_sampler_base(7) <ldms_sampler_base>`,\n:ref:`coretemp(7) <coretemp>`\n",

#ifndef OS_UTIL_H_
#define OS_UTIL_H_

#include <byteswap.h>
#include <endian.h>

static inline long double bswap_long_double(long double value) {
	unsigned long long	v[2];
	unsigned long long	s[2];
	long double		swapped;

	bcopy(&value, v, sizeof(v));

	s[0] = bswap_64(v[0]);
	s[1] = bswap_64(v[1]);

	bcopy(s, &swapped, sizeof(swapped));
	return swapped;
}

Done. Since `coretemp` can never work on Grace nodes, I wrote you a sibling sampler **`thermal_zones`** that reads `/sys/class/thermal`, in exactly the same style as `coretemp.c`.

## What was added

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `ldms/src/sampler/thermal_zones/thermal_zones.c` | The plugin |
| `ldms/src/sampler/thermal_zones/Makefile.am` | Build rules (mirror of coretemp's) |
| `ldms/src/sampler/thermal_zones/ldms-sampler_thermal_zones.rst` | Man page source |
| `ldms/src/sampler/Makefile.am` | +4 lines: `SUBDIRS` entry |
| `configure.ac` | +2 lines: `OPTION_DEFAULT_ENABLE([thermal_zones], …)` and `AC_CONFIG_FILES` entry |

Design (deliberately close to coretemp so your downstream tooling stays familiar):

- **Discovery** at `config` time via `scandir("/sys/class/thermal")`, numeric sort (`thermal_zone2` before `thermal_zone10`), one pass.
- **Schema**: a `zone_temps` record per zone, appended to a `zone_list` list — same record-in-list pattern as `core_temps`/`core_list`:
  - `zone_no` u32 — the `N` in `thermal_zoneN` (your differentiator, since all types are `acpitz`)
  - `type` char[32] — zone type (`acpitz`)
  - `temp` f32 °C — refreshed every sample
  - `trip_temp` f32 °C — highest `trip_point_*_temp` (static, for alerting; `-1` if none)
- Unlike coretemp, it **fails `config` with a clear log message** if no zones are found, instead of silently creating an empty list — and it checks `fopen`/`fread` returns (coretemp segfaults if `tempX_max`/`_crit` files are missing).
- Verified with `gcc -fsyntax-only -Wall` against this tree's headers: clean, and `coretemp.c` passes the same check as a control.

## Build & deploy

On the build host (needs `autogen` since `configure.ac` changed):

```bash
./autogen.sh && ./configure <your usual CSCS flags> && make -C ldms/src/sampler/thermal_zones
# installs as libthermal_zones.so in pkglibdir, next to libcoretemp.so
```

## New conf — `/opt/cscs/ldms/sampler_thermal_zones.conf`

```
load name=thermal_zones
config name=thermal_zones producer=nid006240 instance=nid006240/thermal_zones component_id=6240
start name=thermal_zones interval=10000000 offset=0
```

Expected output:

```
$ ldms_ls -h localhost -p 10004 -l
nid006240/thermal_zones: consistent, last update: ...
M u64          component_id   6240
D u64          job_id         0
D u64          app_id         0
M record_type  zone_temps     LDMS_V_RECORD_TYPE
D list<>       zone_list      [ {zone_no=0, type="acpitz", temp=39.1, trip_temp=...}, ... 52 records ]
```

Two caveats: zone discovery is a one-time scan at `config` (same as coretemp), and the ACPI zone→sensor mapping (which zone is which die) isn't labeled by the firmware — if you need that semantic mapping, it would have to come from NVIDIA's board documentation or BMC/Redfish. And keep `coretemp` out of the Grace node configs; it's harmless but permanently empty there.
